# Task
You will be given a **Requirement** and a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML. Your task is to annotate every operation listed in **Operations** with a specification.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pseudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Take the principle of modularization into account.
2. For each operation, determine its functional boundary based on the given requirement. Ensure high cohesion and low coupling.
3. If there is any unspecified details, such as undefined constants, rates, and ratios, then you must infer them.
4. Make sure that all requirements can be covered.

# Requirement
```text
Employee Management System
The employee management system manages a named company that owns multiple departments and employs workers, salespeople, and managers.
Every employee stores the department label, name, birth date, and social insurance number.
Workers store weekly working hours and hourly rates.
Off-shift workers additionally store whether they have a weekend permit and whether they have an official holiday permit.
Shift workers additionally store a holiday premium and can only belong to the Delivery department.
Salespeople store a fixed salary, an amount of sales, and a commission percentage.
Managers store a fixed salary, a position title, and a list of direct subordinate employees.
Each department is identified by a department type of PRODUCTION, CONTROL, or DELIVERY, stores its employees, and is controlled by one manager.
The company can calculate the total salary of all employees, the total commission amount of all salespeople, and the total holiday premiums paid to all shift workers. Monetary results are rounded to two decimal places.
A department can calculate the average weekly working hours of its workers and returns 0 when the department has no workers. The average is rounded to two decimal places.
A manager can return the number of direct subordinate employees.
Functional requirement:
1. Calculate the total salary of all employees in the company. Total salary equals the sum of workers' salary, salespeople's salary, and managers' salary. Workers' salary equals weeklyWorkingHour multiplied by hourlyRates, plus holiday premiums for shift workers. Salespeople's salary equals salary plus amountOfSales multiplied by commissionPercentage. Managers' salary equals salary. Retain two decimal places for the return value.
2. Calculate the average weekly working hours for all workers within a given department. The method operates on a Department instance and computes the average of all Worker employees' weeklyWorkingHour in that department's employee list. Return 0 if there are no workers in the department. Retain two decimal places for the return value.
3. Determine the total commission amount for all salespeople in the company. Sum amountOfSales multiplied by commissionPercentage for all salespeople. Retain two decimal places for the return value.
4. Calculate the total holiday premiums paid to all shift workers in the company. Shift workers can only belong to the Delivery department; the setDepartment method of ShiftWorker shall throw an IllegalArgumentException when the department is null or not "Delivery". Retain two decimal places for the return value. Return 0 if there are no shift workers in the company.
5. Get the number of direct subordinate employees for each manager.
```

# Class Diagram
```plantuml
@startuml

title employee Model

class Company {
    name : String
    departments : List<Department>
    employees : List<Employee>
    calculateTotalEmployeeSalary() : double
    calculateTotalSalesPeopleCommission() : double
    calculateTotalShiftWorkerHolidayPremiums() : double
}
class Department {
    type : DepartmentType
    company : Company
    employees : List<Employee>
    manager : Manager
    calculateAverageWorkerWorkingHours() : double
}
abstract class Employee {
    birthDate : EDate
    department : String
    name : String
    socialInsuranceNumber : String
    company : Company
}
class Manager {
    position : String
    salary : double
    subordinates : List<Employee>
    getDirectSubordinateEmployeesCount() : int
}
class OffShiftWorker {
    officialHolidayPermit : boolean
    weekendPermit : boolean
}
class SalesPeople {
    amountOfSales : double
    commissionPercentage : double
    salary : double
    getTotalCommission() : double
}
class ShiftWorker {
    holidayPremium : double
    calculateHolidayPremium() : double
    setdepartment(department : String) : void
}
abstract class Worker {
    hourlyRates : double
    weeklyWorkingHour : int
}
enum DepartmentType {
    PRODUCTION
    CONTROL
    DELIVERY
}

Company *-- "*" Department : departments  'opposite: company
Company *-- "*" Employee : employees  'opposite: company
Department --> Company : company  'opposite: departments
Department --> "*" Employee : employees
Department --> Manager : manager
Employee --> Company : company  'opposite: employees
Manager --|> Employee
Manager --> "*" Employee : subordinates
OffShiftWorker --|> Worker
SalesPeople --|> Employee
ShiftWorker --|> Worker
Worker --|> Employee
@enduml
```

{org.eclipse.emf.ecore.impl.EOperationImpl@697d6ded (name: calculateAverageWorkerWorkingHours) (ordered: true, unique: true, lowerBound: 0, upperBound: 1)=Summary: Calculates the average weekly working hours of all workers assigned to this department.
Algorithm: 
  1. Initialize sumHours = 0, workerCount = 0
  2. For each emp in employees:
     If emp is instance of Worker:
        sumHours += emp.weeklyWorkingHour
        workerCount++
  3. If workerCount == 0, return 0.0
  4. average = sumHours / workerCount
  5. Round average to two decimal places.
  6. Return average.
Related Features: employees, Worker.weeklyWorkingHour
Related Classes: Employee, Worker

Output: double representing the average weekly working hours, rounded to two decimal places. Value range: [0, ∞)

Post-condition: The system state remains unchanged.   2. For each emp in employees:
     If emp is instance of ShiftWorker:

Related Classes: Employee, ShiftWorker

# Task
You will be given a **Requirement**, a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML, and a list of operation specifications. Your task is to correct the specifications that are inconsistent with the requirement.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pseudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Read the requirement and the class diagram.
2. For each operation specification, determine whether it is complete and consistent with the requirement.
3. If there is any error, correct the erroneous specification.
4. Follow the specification format. Output changed specifications only; OMIT unchanged specifications.

# Requirement
```text
Employee Management System
The employee management system manages a named company that owns multiple departments and employs workers, salespeople, and managers.
Every employee stores the department label, name, birth date, and social insurance number.
Workers store weekly working hours and hourly rates.
Off-shift workers additionally store whether they have a weekend permit and whether they have an official holiday permit.
Shift workers additionally store a holiday premium and can only belong to the Delivery department.
Salespeople store a fixed salary, an amount of sales, and a commission percentage.
Managers store a fixed salary, a position title, and a list of direct subordinate employees.
Each department is identified by a department type of PRODUCTION, CONTROL, or DELIVERY, stores its employees, and is controlled by one manager.
The company can calculate the total salary of all employees, the total commission amount of all salespeople, and the total holiday premiums paid to all shift workers. Monetary results are rounded to two decimal places.
A department can calculate the average weekly working hours of its workers and returns 0 when the department has no workers. The average is rounded to two decimal places.
A manager can return the number of direct subordinate employees.
Functional requirement:
1. Calculate the total salary of all employees in the company. Total salary equals the sum of workers' salary, salespeople's salary, and managers' salary. Workers' salary equals weeklyWorkingHour multiplied by hourlyRates, plus holiday premiums for shift workers. Salespeople's salary equals salary plus amountOfSales multiplied by commissionPercentage. Managers' salary equals salary. Retain two decimal places for the return value.
2. Calculate the average weekly working hours for all workers within a given department. The method operates on a Department instance and computes the average of all Worker employees' weeklyWorkingHour in that department's employee list. Return 0 if there are no workers in the department. Retain two decimal places for the return value.
3. Determine the total commission amount for all salespeople in the company. Sum amountOfSales multiplied by commissionPercentage for all salespeople. Retain two decimal places for the return value.
4. Calculate the total holiday premiums paid to all shift workers in the company. Shift workers can only belong to the Delivery department; the setDepartment method of ShiftWorker shall throw an IllegalArgumentException when the department is null or not "Delivery". Retain two decimal places for the return value. Return 0 if there are no shift workers in the company.
5. Get the number of direct subordinate employees for each manager.
```

# Class Diagram
```plantuml

}

    officialHolidayPermit : boolean
    weekendPermit : boolean
}

    holidayPremium : double
    calculateHolidayPremium() : double

    hourlyRates : double

    DELIVERY
```

# Operation Specifications
Company::calculateTotalShiftWorkerHolidayPremiums
Summary: Computes the total holiday premiums allocated to all shift workers within the company.
Algorithm: 
  1. Initialize totalPremium = 0.0
  2. For each emp in employees:
     If emp is instance of ShiftWorker:
        totalPremium += emp.holidayPremium
  3. Round totalPremium to two decimal places.
  4. Return totalPremium (returns 0.0 if no shift workers exist).
Related Features: employees, ShiftWorker.holidayPremium
Related Classes: Employee, ShiftWorker
Input: None
Output: double representing the total holiday premium amount, rounded to two decimal places. Value range: [0, ∞)
Pre-condition: The employees list is initialized and contains only valid Employee instances.
Post-condition: The system state remains unchanged. The returned value equals the sum of holiday premiums for all shift workers, formatted to two decimal places.

Company::calculateTotalEmployeeSalary
Summary: Calculates the total salary for all employees across the company by aggregating salaries from workers, salespeople, and managers.
Algorithm: 
  1. Initialize totalSalary = 0.0
  2. For each emp in employees:
     a. If emp is instance of Worker:
        basePay = emp.weeklyWorkingHour * emp.hourlyRates
        If emp is instance of ShiftWorker: basePay += emp.holidayPremium
        totalSalary += basePay
     b. If emp is instance of SalesPeople:
        totalSalary += emp.salary + (emp.amountOfSales * emp.commissionPercentage)
     c. If emp is instance of Manager:
        totalSalary += emp.salary
  3. Round totalSalary to two decimal places.
  4. Return totalSalary.
Related Features: employees, Worker.weeklyWorkingHour, Worker.hourlyRates, ShiftWorker.holidayPremium, SalesPeople.salary, SalesPeople.amountOfSales, SalesPeople.commissionPercentage, Manager.salary
Related Classes: Employee, Worker, ShiftWorker, SalesPeople, Manager
Input: None
Output: double representing the aggregated total salary, rounded to two decimal places. Value range: [0, ∞)
Pre-condition: The employees list is initialized and contains only valid Employee instances.
Post-condition: The system state remains unchanged. The returned value equals the sum of individual employee salaries as defined, formatted to two decimal places.

Company::calculateTotalSalesPeopleCommission
Summary: Computes the total commission amount generated by all salespeople employed by the company.
Algorithm: 
  1. Initialize totalCommission = 0.0
  2. For each emp in employees:
     If emp is instance of SalesPeople:
        totalCommission += emp.amountOfSales * emp.commissionPercentage
  3. Round totalCommission to two decimal places.
  4. Return totalCommission.
Related Features: employees, SalesPeople.amountOfSales, SalesPeople.commissionPercentage
Related Classes: Employee, SalesPeople
Input: None
Output: double representing the total commission amount, rounded to two decimal places. Value range: [0, ∞)
Pre-condition: The employees list is initialized and contains only valid Employee instances.
Post-condition: The system state remains unchanged. The returned value equals the sum of commissions for all salespeople, formatted to two decimal places.

```text
4. Calculate the total holiday premiums paid to all shift workers in the company. Shift workers can only belong to the Delivery department; the setDepartment method of ShiftWorker shall throw an IllegalArgumentException when the department is null or not "Delivery". Retain two decimal places for the return value. Return 0 if there are no shift workers in the company.
5. Get the number of direct subordinate employees for each manager.
```

# Requirement
```text
Employee Management System
The employee management system manages a named company that owns multiple departments and employs workers, salespeople, and managers.
Every employee stores the department label, name, birth date, and social insurance number.
Workers store weekly working hours and hourly rates.
Off-shift workers additionally store whether they have a weekend permit and whether they have an official holiday permit.
Shift workers additionally store a holiday premium and can only belong to the Delivery department.
Salespeople store a fixed salary, an amount of sales, and a commission percentage.
Managers store a fixed salary, a position title, and a list of direct subordinate employees.
Each department is identified by a department type of PRODUCTION, CONTROL, or DELIVERY, stores its employees, and is controlled by one manager.
The company can calculate the total salary of all employees, the total commission amount of all salespeople, and the total holiday premiums paid to all shift workers. Monetary results are rounded to two decimal places.
A department can calculate the average weekly working hours of its workers and returns 0 when the department has no workers. The average is rounded to two decimal places.
A manager can return the number of direct subordinate employees.
Functional requirement:
1. Calculate the total salary of all employees in the company. Total salary equals the sum of workers' salary, salespeople's salary, and managers' salary. Workers' salary equals weeklyWorkingHour multiplied by hourlyRates, plus holiday premiums for shift workers. Salespeople's salary equals salary plus amountOfSales multiplied by commissionPercentage. Managers' salary equals salary. Retain two decimal places for the return value.
2. Calculate the average weekly working hours for all workers within a given department. The method operates on a Department instance and computes the average of all Worker employees' weeklyWorkingHour in that department's employee list. Return 0 if there are no workers in the department. Retain two decimal places for the return value.
3. Determine the total commission amount for all salespeople in the company. Sum amountOfSales multiplied by commissionPercentage for all salespeople. Retain two decimal places for the return value.
4. Calculate the total holiday premiums paid to all shift workers in the company. Shift workers can only belong to the Delivery department; the setDepartment method of ShiftWorker shall throw an IllegalArgumentException when the department is null or not "Delivery". Retain two decimal places for the return value. Return 0 if there are no shift workers in the company.
5. Get the number of direct subordinate employees for each manager.
```

```plantuml
    type : DepartmentType
    company : Company
    employees : List<Employee>
    manager : Manager
    calculateAverageWorkerWorkingHours() : double
}

    department : String

    weekendPermit : boolean
```

# Operation Specifications
Department::calculateAverageWorkerWorkingHours
Summary: Calculates the average weekly working hours of all workers assigned to this department.
Algorithm: 
  1. Initialize sumHours = 0, workerCount = 0
  2. For each emp in employees:
     If emp is instance of Worker:
        sumHours += emp.weeklyWorkingHour
        workerCount++
  3. If workerCount == 0, return 0.0
  4. average = sumHours / workerCount
  5. Round average to two decimal places.
  6. Return average.
Related Features: employees, Worker.weeklyWorkingHour
Related Classes: Employee, Worker
Input: None
Output: double representing the average weekly working hours, rounded to two decimal places. Value range: [0, ∞)
Pre-condition: The employees list is initialized and contains only valid Employee instances.
Post-condition: The system state remains unchanged. The returned value equals the arithmetic mean of weekly working hours for workers in the department, formatted to two decimal places.

# Requirement
```text
Employee Management System
The employee management system manages a named company that owns multiple departments and employs workers, salespeople, and managers.
Every employee stores the department label, name, birth date, and social insurance number.
Workers store weekly working hours and hourly rates.
Off-shift workers additionally store whether they have a weekend permit and whether they have an official holiday permit.
Shift workers additionally store a holiday premium and can only belong to the Delivery department.
Salespeople store a fixed salary, an amount of sales, and a commission percentage.
Managers store a fixed salary, a position title, and a list of direct subordinate employees.
Each department is identified by a department type of PRODUCTION, CONTROL, or DELIVERY, stores its employees, and is controlled by one manager.
The company can calculate the total salary of all employees, the total commission amount of all salespeople, and the total holiday premiums paid to all shift workers. Monetary results are rounded to two decimal places.
A department can calculate the average weekly working hours of its workers and returns 0 when the department has no workers. The average is rounded to two decimal places.
A manager can return the number of direct subordinate employees.
Functional requirement:
1. Calculate the total salary of all employees in the company. Total salary equals the sum of workers' salary, salespeople's salary, and managers' salary. Workers' salary equals weeklyWorkingHour multiplied by hourlyRates, plus holiday premiums for shift workers. Salespeople's salary equals salary plus amountOfSales multiplied by commissionPercentage. Managers' salary equals salary. Retain two decimal places for the return value.
2. Calculate the average weekly working hours for all workers within a given department. The method operates on a Department instance and computes the average of all Worker employees' weeklyWorkingHour in that department's employee list. Return 0 if there are no workers in the department. Retain two decimal places for the return value.
3. Determine the total commission amount for all salespeople in the company. Sum amountOfSales multiplied by commissionPercentage for all salespeople. Retain two decimal places for the return value.
4. Calculate the total holiday premiums paid to all shift workers in the company. Shift workers can only belong to the Delivery department; the setDepartment method of ShiftWorker shall throw an IllegalArgumentException when the department is null or not "Delivery". Retain two decimal places for the return value. Return 0 if there are no shift workers in the company.
5. Get the number of direct subordinate employees for each manager.
```

# Task
You will be given a **Java Class** generated from an Ecore model by EMF code generator with `suppressInterfaces=true`. Your task is to implement the Java methods unimplemented by taking additional contextual information into account.

Complete this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Compute context information
  1.1 Consider the related classes in the Javadocs.
  1.2 Locate these files in your project.
  1.3 Generate the class summarization.
2. A method is unimplemented if its body contains `// TODO: Please complete this Java method based on the docstring`.
3. For every unimplemented method, 
  3.1 You must read the docstring of this method. The fragment between `<!-- begin-model-doc -->` and `<!-- end-model-doc -->` contains the method specification;
  3.2 Generate your code strictly according to the algorithm; Be aware of the EMF code style and APIs;
  3.3 You should not change the method signature;
  3.4 After generation, replace the current body with the one you generate;
  3.5 Keep and update the docstring by changing `@generated` to `@generated NOT`.
4. Check the import list. Add missing imports if needed.
5. Do not change other part.
6. Be aware of the method signatures, particularly, the input and output types, provided in the context.

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.
- When possibe, use the creation factory for object creation.
- To create an EList instance, import and use `org.eclipse.emf.common.util.BasicEList`.

```java
   * Summary: Calculates the average weekly working hours of all workers assigned to this department.
   * Algorithm: 
   *   1. Initialize sumHours = 0, workerCount = 0

   *         sumHours += emp.weeklyWorkingHour
```

   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public double calculateAverageWorkerWorkingHours()
  {
    double sumHours = 0.0;
    int workerCount = 0;
    for (Employee emp : getEmployees()) {
      if (emp instanceof Worker) {
        sumHours += ((Worker) emp).getWeeklyWorkingHour();
        workerCount++;
      }
    }
    if (workerCount == 0) {
      return 0.0;
    }
    double average = sumHours / workerCount;
    return Math.round(average * 100.0) / 100.0;
  }

   * Summary: Calculates the total salary for all employees across the company by aggregating salaries from workers, salespeople, and managers.
   * Algorithm: 
   *   1. Initialize totalSalary = 0.0
   *   2. For each emp in employees:
   *      a. If emp is instance of Worker:
   *         basePay = emp.weeklyWorkingHour * emp.hourlyRates
   *         If emp is instance of ShiftWorker: basePay += emp.holidayPremium
   *         totalSalary += basePay
   *      b. If emp is instance of SalesPeople:
   *         totalSalary += emp.salary + (emp.amountOfSales * emp.commissionPercentage)
   *      c. If emp is instance of Manager:
   *         totalSalary += emp.salary
   *   3. totalSalary = Math.round(totalSalary * 100) / 100.0
   *   4. Return totalSalary.
   * Related Features: employees, Worker.weeklyWorkingHour, Worker.hourlyRates, ShiftWorker.holidayPremium, SalesPeople.salary, SalesPeople.amountOfSales, SalesPeople.commissionPercentage, Manager.salary
   * Related Classes: Employee, Worker, ShiftWorker, SalesPeople, Manager
   * Input: None
   * Output: double representing the aggregated total salary, rounded to two decimal places. Value range: [0, ∞)
   * Pre-condition: The employees list is initialized and contains only valid Employee instances.
   * Post-condition: The system state remains unchanged. The returned value equals the sum of individual employee salaries as defined, formatted to two decimal places.
   * <!-- end-model-doc -->
   * @model annotation="http://www.ustb.edu.cn/sei/mde/EnhancedCodeGen generated='true'"
   * @generated NOT
   */
  public double calculateTotalEmployeeSalary()
  {
    double totalSalary = 0.0;
    for (Employee emp : getEmployees())
    {
        if (emp instanceof Worker)
        {
            double basePay = emp.getWeeklyWorkingHour() * emp.getHourlyRates();
            if (emp instanceof ShiftWorker)
            {
                basePay += emp.getHolidayPremium();
            }
            totalSalary += basePay;
        }
        else if (emp instanceof SalesPeople)
        {
            totalSalary += emp.getSalary() + (emp.getAmountOfSales() * emp.getCommissionPercentage());
        }
        else if (emp instanceof Manager)
        {
            totalSalary += emp.getSalary();
        }
    }
    return Math.round(totalSalary * 100) / 100.0;
  }

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.

```java
  {
    double totalSalary = 0.0;
    for (Employee emp : getEmployees())
    {
        if (emp instanceof Worker)
        {
            double basePay = emp.getWeeklyWorkingHour() * emp.getHourlyRates();
            if (emp instanceof ShiftWorker)
            {
                basePay += emp.getHolidayPremium();
            }
            totalSalary += basePay;

        else if (emp instanceof SalesPeople)

        }
        else if (emp instanceof Manager)
        {
            totalSalary += emp.getSalary();
        }
    }
    return Math.round(totalSalary * 100) / 100.0;
  }
```

# Task
You will be given a **Requirement** and a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML. Your task is to annotate every operation listed in **Operations** with a specification.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pseudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Take the principle of modularization into account.
2. For each operation, determine its functional boundary based on the given requirement. Ensure high cohesion and low coupling.
3. If there is any unspecified details, such as undefined constants, rates, and ratios, then you must infer them.
4. Make sure that all requirements can be covered.

# Requirement
```text
Employee Management System
The employee management system manages a named company that owns multiple departments and employs workers, salespeople, and managers.
Every employee stores the department label, name, birth date, and social insurance number.
Workers store weekly working hours and hourly rates.
Off-shift workers additionally store whether they have a weekend permit and whether they have an official holiday permit.
Shift workers additionally store a holiday premium and can only belong to the Delivery department.
Salespeople store a fixed salary, an amount of sales, and a commission percentage.
Managers store a fixed salary, a position title, and a list of direct subordinate employees.
Each department is identified by a department type of PRODUCTION, CONTROL, or DELIVERY, stores its employees, and is controlled by one manager.
The company can calculate the total salary of all employees, the total commission amount of all salespeople, and the total holiday premiums paid to all shift workers. Monetary results are rounded to two decimal places.
A department can calculate the average weekly working hours of its workers and returns 0 when the department has no workers. The average is rounded to two decimal places.
A manager can return the number of direct subordinate employees.
Functional requirement:
1. Calculate the total salary of all employees in the company. Total salary equals the sum of workers' salary, salespeople's salary, and managers' salary. Workers' salary equals weeklyWorkingHour multiplied by hourlyRates, plus holiday premiums for shift workers. Salespeople's salary equals salary plus amountOfSales multiplied by commissionPercentage. Managers' salary equals salary. Retain two decimal places for the return value.
2. Calculate the average weekly working hours for all workers within a given department. The method operates on a Department instance and computes the average of all Worker employees' weeklyWorkingHour in that department's employee list. Return 0 if there are no workers in the department. Retain two decimal places for the return value.
3. Determine the total commission amount for all salespeople in the company. Sum amountOfSales multiplied by commissionPercentage for all salespeople. Retain two decimal places for the return value.
4. Calculate the total holiday premiums paid to all shift workers in the company. Shift workers can only belong to the Delivery department; the setDepartment method of ShiftWorker shall throw an IllegalArgumentException when the department is null or not "Delivery". Retain two decimal places for the return value. Return 0 if there are no shift workers in the company.
5. Get the number of direct subordinate employees for each manager.
```

# Class Diagram
```plantuml
@startuml

title employee Model

class Company {
    name : String
    departments : List<Department>
    employees : List<Employee>
    calculateTotalEmployeeSalary() : double
    calculateTotalSalesPeopleCommission() : double
    calculateTotalShiftWorkerHolidayPremiums() : double
}
class Department {
    type : DepartmentType
    company : Company
    employees : List<Employee>
    manager : Manager
    calculateAverageWorkerWorkingHours() : double
}
abstract class Employee {
    birthDate : EDate
    department : String
    name : String
    socialInsuranceNumber : String
    company : Company
}
class Manager {
    position : String
    salary : double
    subordinates : List<Employee>
    getDirectSubordinateEmployeesCount() : int
}
class OffShiftWorker {
    officialHolidayPermit : boolean
    weekendPermit : boolean
}
class SalesPeople {
    amountOfSales : double
    commissionPercentage : double
    salary : double
    getTotalCommission() : double
}
class ShiftWorker {
    holidayPremium : double
    calculateHolidayPremium() : double
    setdepartment(department : String) : void
}
abstract class Worker {
    hourlyRates : double
    weeklyWorkingHour : int
}
enum DepartmentType {
    PRODUCTION
    CONTROL
    DELIVERY
}

Company *-- "*" Department : departments  'opposite: company
Company *-- "*" Employee : employees  'opposite: company
Department --> Company : company  'opposite: departments
Department --> "*" Employee : employees
Department --> Manager : manager
Employee --> Company : company  'opposite: employees
Manager --|> Employee
Manager --> "*" Employee : subordinates
OffShiftWorker --|> Worker
SalesPeople --|> Employee
ShiftWorker --|> Worker
Worker --|> Employee
@enduml
```

# Task
You will be given a **Requirement**, a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML, and a list of operation specifications. Your task is to correct the specifications that are inconsistent with the requirement.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pseudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Read the requirement and the class diagram.
2. For each operation specification, determine whether it is complete and consistent with the requirement.
3. If there is any error, correct the erroneous specification.
4. Follow the specification format. Output changed specifications only; OMIT unchanged specifications.

# Requirement
```text
Employee Management System
The employee management system manages a named company that owns multiple departments and employs workers, salespeople, and managers.
Every employee stores the department label, name, birth date, and social insurance number.
Workers store weekly working hours and hourly rates.
Off-shift workers additionally store whether they have a weekend permit and whether they have an official holiday permit.
Shift workers additionally store a holiday premium and can only belong to the Delivery department.
Salespeople store a fixed salary, an amount of sales, and a commission percentage.
Managers store a fixed salary, a position title, and a list of direct subordinate employees.
Each department is identified by a department type of PRODUCTION, CONTROL, or DELIVERY, stores its employees, and is controlled by one manager.
The company can calculate the total salary of all employees, the total commission amount of all salespeople, and the total holiday premiums paid to all shift workers. Monetary results are rounded to two decimal places.
A department can calculate the average weekly working hours of its workers and returns 0 when the department has no workers. The average is rounded to two decimal places.
A manager can return the number of direct subordinate employees.
Functional requirement:
1. Calculate the total salary of all employees in the company. Total salary equals the sum of workers' salary, salespeople's salary, and managers' salary. Workers' salary equals weeklyWorkingHour multiplied by hourlyRates, plus holiday premiums for shift workers. Salespeople's salary equals salary plus amountOfSales multiplied by commissionPercentage. Managers' salary equals salary. Retain two decimal places for the return value.
2. Calculate the average weekly working hours for all workers within a given department. The method operates on a Department instance and computes the average of all Worker employees' weeklyWorkingHour in that department's employee list. Return 0 if there are no workers in the department. Retain two decimal places for the return value.
3. Determine the total commission amount for all salespeople in the company. Sum amountOfSales multiplied by commissionPercentage for all salespeople. Retain two decimal places for the return value.
4. Calculate the total holiday premiums paid to all shift workers in the company. Shift workers can only belong to the Delivery department; the setDepartment method of ShiftWorker shall throw an IllegalArgumentException when the department is null or not "Delivery". Retain two decimal places for the return value. Return 0 if there are no shift workers in the company.
5. Get the number of direct subordinate employees for each manager.
```

```plantuml
    weekendPermit : boolean
```

# Operation Specifications
Company::calculateTotalShiftWorkerHolidayPremiums
1. Summary: Calculates the cumulative holiday premium payments distributed to all ShiftWorkers within the company.
2. Algorithm: 
   1. Initialize totalPremium = 0.0.
   2. Iterate through each employee in the employees list.
   3. If the employee is an instance of ShiftWorker, add calculateHolidayPremium() to totalPremium.
   4. If no ShiftWorkers are found (totalPremium remains 0.0), return 0.0.
   5. Apply rounding to two decimal places: Math.round(totalPremium * 100.0) / 100.0.
   6. Return the rounded result.
3. Related Features: employees, holidayPremium, calculateHolidayPremium()
4. Related Classes: Employee, ShiftWorker
5. Input: None
6. Output: double - The total holiday premiums paid to shift workers, formatted to exactly two decimal places. Returns 0.0 if no shift workers exist.
7. Pre-condition: The employees list is not null. All ShiftWorker instances have valid holidayPremium values.
8. Post-condition: Returns the aggregated premium amount. System state remains unaltered. Result is strictly bounded to two decimal precision.

Company::calculateTotalEmployeeSalary
1. Summary: Aggregates and returns the total salary payable to all employees registered in the company by combining individual compensation components across Workers, Salespeople, and Managers.
2. Algorithm: 
   1. Initialize totalSalary = 0.0.
   2. Iterate through each employee in the employees list.
   3. Determine employee type:
      - If Worker: baseSal = weeklyWorkingHour * hourlyRates. If ShiftWorker, baseSal += calculateHolidayPremium().
      - If SalesPeople: baseSal = salary + getTotalCommission().
      - If Manager: baseSal = salary.
   4. totalSalary += baseSal.
   5. Apply rounding to two decimal places: Math.round(totalSalary * 100.0) / 100.0.
   6. Return the rounded result.
3. Related Features: employees, weeklyWorkingHour, hourlyRates, holidayPremium, salary, amountOfSales, commissionPercentage, calculateHolidayPremium(), getTotalCommission()
4. Related Classes: Employee, Worker, ShiftWorker, OffShiftWorker, SalesPeople, Manager
5. Input: None
6. Output: double - The total salary of all company employees, formatted to exactly two decimal places.
7. Pre-condition: The employees list is not null and contains valid, fully initialized employee instances. Financial attributes (rates, salaries, hours) are non-negative.
8. Post-condition: Returns the precise sum of all individual employee salaries, strictly rounded to two decimal places. No internal state of the Company or its employees is modified.

Company::calculateTotalSalesPeopleCommission
1. Summary: Computes the aggregate commission earnings generated by all Salespeople employed by the company.
2. Algorithm: 
   1. Initialize totalCommission = 0.0.
   2. Iterate through each employee in the employees list.
   3. If the employee is an instance of SalesPeople, add getTotalCommission() to totalCommission.
   4. Apply rounding to two decimal places: Math.round(totalCommission * 100.0) / 100.0.
   5. Return the rounded result.
3. Related Features: employees, amountOfSales, commissionPercentage, getTotalCommission()
4. Related Classes: Employee, SalesPeople
5. Input: None
6. Output: double - The total commission amount for all salespeople, formatted to exactly two decimal places.
7. Pre-condition: The employees list is not null. All referenced SalesPeople objects are properly instantiated.
8. Post-condition: Returns the summed commission values across all sales staff. Company state remains completely unchanged. Result is non-negative and rounded to two decimal places.

      - If instance of Manager: baseSal = salary.
   4. totalSalary += baseSal.
   5. Apply rounding to two decimal places: Math.round(totalSalary * 100.0) / 100.0.
   6. Return the rounded result.
3. Related Features: employees, weeklyWorkingHour, hourlyRates, salary, calculateHolidayPremium(), getTotalCommission()
4. Related Classes: Employee, Worker, ShiftWorker, OffShiftWorker, SalesPeople, Manager
5. Input: None
6. Output: double - The total salary of all company employees, formatted to exactly two decimal places.
7. Pre-condition: The employees list is not null and contains valid, fully initialized employee instances. Financial attributes (rates, salaries, hours) are non-negative.
8. Post-condition: Returns the precise sum of all individual employee salaries, strictly rounded to two decimal places. No internal state of the Company or its employees is modified., org.eclipse.emf.ecore.impl.EOperationImpl@2b6a4da7 (name: calculateTotalSalesPeopleCommission) (ordered: true, unique: true, lowerBound: 0, upperBound: 1)=1. Summary: Computes the aggregate commission earnings generated by all Salespeople employed by the company.
2. Algorithm: 
   1. Initialize totalCommission = 0.0.
   2. Iterate through each employee in the employees list.
   3. If the employee is an instance of SalesPeople, add getTotalCommission() to totalCommission.
   4. Apply rounding to two decimal places: Math.round(totalCommission * 100.0) / 100.0.
   5. Return the rounded result.
3. Related Features: employees, getTotalCommission()
4. Related Classes: Employee, SalesPeople
5. Input: None
6. Output: double - The total commission amount for all salespeople, formatted to exactly two decimal places.
7. Pre-condition: The employees list is not null. All referenced SalesPeople objects are properly instantiated.
8. Post-condition: Returns the summed commission values across all sales staff. Company state remains completely unchanged. Result is non-negative and rounded to two decimal places.}

# Requirement
```text
Employee Management System
The employee management system manages a named company that owns multiple departments and employs workers, salespeople, and managers.
Every employee stores the department label, name, birth date, and social insurance number.
Workers store weekly working hours and hourly rates.
Off-shift workers additionally store whether they have a weekend permit and whether they have an official holiday permit.
Shift workers additionally store a holiday premium and can only belong to the Delivery department.
Salespeople store a fixed salary, an amount of sales, and a commission percentage.
Managers store a fixed salary, a position title, and a list of direct subordinate employees.
Each department is identified by a department type of PRODUCTION, CONTROL, or DELIVERY, stores its employees, and is controlled by one manager.
The company can calculate the total salary of all employees, the total commission amount of all salespeople, and the total holiday premiums paid to all shift workers. Monetary results are rounded to two decimal places.
A department can calculate the average weekly working hours of its workers and returns 0 when the department has no workers. The average is rounded to two decimal places.
A manager can return the number of direct subordinate employees.
Functional requirement:
1. Calculate the total salary of all employees in the company. Total salary equals the sum of workers' salary, salespeople's salary, and managers' salary. Workers' salary equals weeklyWorkingHour multiplied by hourlyRates, plus holiday premiums for shift workers. Salespeople's salary equals salary plus amountOfSales multiplied by commissionPercentage. Managers' salary equals salary. Retain two decimal places for the return value.
2. Calculate the average weekly working hours for all workers within a given department. The method operates on a Department instance and computes the average of all Worker employees' weeklyWorkingHour in that department's employee list. Return 0 if there are no workers in the department. Retain two decimal places for the return value.
3. Determine the total commission amount for all salespeople in the company. Sum amountOfSales multiplied by commissionPercentage for all salespeople. Retain two decimal places for the return value.
4. Calculate the total holiday premiums paid to all shift workers in the company. Shift workers can only belong to the Delivery department; the setDepartment method of ShiftWorker shall throw an IllegalArgumentException when the department is null or not "Delivery". Retain two decimal places for the return value. Return 0 if there are no shift workers in the company.
5. Get the number of direct subordinate employees for each manager.
```

# Operation Specifications
Department::calculateAverageWorkerWorkingHours
1. Summary: Computes the arithmetic mean of weekly working hours for all Worker-type employees assigned to this department.
2. Algorithm: 
   1. Filter the employees list to extract only instances of Worker.
   2. Let count = size of filtered list.
   3. If count == 0, return 0.0 immediately.
   4. Calculate sumHours = sum(emp.weeklyWorkingHour for each emp in filtered list).
   5. Compute average = sumHours / count.
   6. Apply rounding to two decimal places: Math.round(average * 100.0) / 100.0.
   7. Return the rounded result.
3. Related Features: employees, weeklyWorkingHour
4. Related Classes: Employee, Worker
5. Input: None
6. Output: double - The average weekly working hours of workers in the department, formatted to exactly two decimal places. Returns 0.0 if the department employs no workers.
7. Pre-condition: The employees list is not null. All Worker instances have initialized weeklyWorkingHour values.
8. Post-condition: Returns the computed average. Department state remains unmodified. Output is non-negative and rounded to two decimal places.

```result
 * revised description for operation_1
 */

/*
 * revised description for this operation_2
 */

...
```

3. Related Features: employees, weeklyWorkingHour
4. Related Classes: Employee, Worker
5. Input: None
6. Output: double - The average weekly working hours of workers in the department, formatted to exactly two decimal places. Returns 0.0 if the department employs no workers.
7. Pre-condition: The employees list is not null. All Worker instances have initialized weeklyWorkingHour values.
8. Post-condition: Returns the computed average. Department state remains unmodified. Output is non-negative and rounded to two decimal places.

# Requirement
```text
Employee Management System
The employee management system manages a named company that owns multiple departments and employs workers, salespeople, and managers.
Every employee stores the department label, name, birth date, and social insurance number.
Workers store weekly working hours and hourly rates.
Off-shift workers additionally store whether they have a weekend permit and whether they have an official holiday permit.
Shift workers additionally store a holiday premium and can only belong to the Delivery department.
Salespeople store a fixed salary, an amount of sales, and a commission percentage.
Managers store a fixed salary, a position title, and a list of direct subordinate employees.
Each department is identified by a department type of PRODUCTION, CONTROL, or DELIVERY, stores its employees, and is controlled by one manager.
The company can calculate the total salary of all employees, the total commission amount of all salespeople, and the total holiday premiums paid to all shift workers. Monetary results are rounded to two decimal places.
A department can calculate the average weekly working hours of its workers and returns 0 when the department has no workers. The average is rounded to two decimal places.
A manager can return the number of direct subordinate employees.
Functional requirement:
1. Calculate the total salary of all employees in the company. Total salary equals the sum of workers' salary, salespeople's salary, and managers' salary. Workers' salary equals weeklyWorkingHour multiplied by hourlyRates, plus holiday premiums for shift workers. Salespeople's salary equals salary plus amountOfSales multiplied by commissionPercentage. Managers' salary equals salary. Retain two decimal places for the return value.
2. Calculate the average weekly working hours for all workers within a given department. The method operates on a Department instance and computes the average of all Worker employees' weeklyWorkingHour in that department's employee list. Return 0 if there are no workers in the department. Retain two decimal places for the return value.
3. Determine the total commission amount for all salespeople in the company. Sum amountOfSales multiplied by commissionPercentage for all salespeople. Retain two decimal places for the return value.
4. Calculate the total holiday premiums paid to all shift workers in the company. Shift workers can only belong to the Delivery department; the setDepartment method of ShiftWorker shall throw an IllegalArgumentException when the department is null or not "Delivery". Retain two decimal places for the return value. Return 0 if there are no shift workers in the company.
5. Get the number of direct subordinate employees for each manager.
```

```plantuml
    weekendPermit : boolean

    DELIVERY
```

SalesPeople::getTotalCommission
1. Summary: Calculates the individual commission payout for this salesperson based on sales volume and agreed commission rate.

   1. Compute rawCommission = amountOfSales * commissionPercentage.

   3. Return the rounded result.
3. Related Features: amountOfSales, commissionPercentage
4. Related Classes: None

7. Pre-condition: amountOfSales >= 0 and commissionPercentage >= 0. Both attributes are initialized and hold valid numeric values.
8. Post-condition: Returns the computed commission value. Instance state remains completely unchanged.

```plantuml
SalesPeople --|> Employee
ShiftWorker --|> Worker
Worker --|> Employee
@enduml
```

- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.
- When possibe, use the creation factory for object creation.
- To create an EList instance, import and use `org.eclipse.emf.common.util.BasicEList`.

     int count = 0;
     double sumHours = 0.0;
     
     for (Employee emp : employees) {
        if (emp instanceof Worker) {
           count++;
           sumHours += emp.getWeeklyWorkingHour();
        }

     if (count == 0) {

     }
     
     double average = sumHours / (double) count;

```java
   *       - If instance of SalesPeople: baseSal = salary + getTotalCommission().

   * 4. Related Classes: Employee, Worker, ShiftWorker, OffShiftWorker, SalesPeople, Manager
```

   * @generated NOT
   */
  public double calculateTotalEmployeeSalary()
  {
    double totalSalary = 0.0;
    for (Employee employee : getEmployees()) {
        double baseSal = 0.0;
        if (employee instanceof Worker) {
            Worker worker = (Worker) employee;
            baseSal = worker.getWeeklyWorkingHour() * worker.getHourlyRates();
            if (employee instanceof ShiftWorker) {
                ShiftWorker shiftWorker = (ShiftWorker) employee;
                baseSal += shiftWorker.calculateHolidayPremium();
            }
        } else if (employee instanceof SalesPeople) {
            SalesPeople salesPerson = (SalesPeople) employee;
            baseSal = salesPerson.getSalary() + salesPerson.getTotalCommission();
        } else if (employee instanceof Manager) {
            Manager manager = (Manager) employee;
            baseSal = manager.getSalary();
        }
        totalSalary += baseSal;
    }
    return Math.round(totalSalary * 100.0) / 100.0;
  }

```java
   * <!-- end-user-doc -->
   * <!-- begin-model-doc -->
   * 1. Summary: Computes the arithmetic mean of weekly working hours for all Worker-type employees assigned to this department.
   * 2. Algorithm: 
   *    1. Filter the employees list to extract only instances of Worker.
   *    2. Let count = size of filtered list.
   *    3. If count == 0, return 0.0 immediately.
   *    4. Calculate sumHours = sum(emp.weeklyWorkingHour for each emp in filtered list).

     double sumHours = 0.0;

           sumHours += emp.getWeeklyWorkingHour();
```

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
